<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We construct in ZFC a unital infinite-dimensional simple complex <i>C</i><sup>∗</sup>-algebra with a faithful tracial state whose nonzero irreducible representations are all unitarily equivalent. This gives a negative answer to Naimark's problem without additional set-theoretic assumptions.

- **claim-naimark-counterexample-zfc** (theorem, [source](#stmt-na-main)): There exists a unital infinite-dimensional simple complex $C^*$-algebra $A$ with a faithful tracial state such that all its nonzero irreducible representations are unitarily equivalent. In particular, $A$ is not isomorphic to $K(H)$ for any complex Hilbert space $H$.
  - Explanation: The main result: in ZFC there is a unital simple infinite-dimensional algebra with a faithful trace whose nonzero irreducible representations form a single equivalence class. It is therefore not a compact-operator algebra, which answers Naimark's problem negatively.
- **claim-unique-lift-pure** (theorem, [source](#stmt-na-unique-lift)): If a pure state $h$ of $B$ has a unique state extension $\widetilde h$ to $C\supset B$, then $\widetilde h$ is pure.
  - Explanation: If a pure state has exactly one state extension to a larger algebra, that extension is itself pure, because any convex decomposition restricts to the same pure state on the base.
- **claim-unitary-conjugate-states** (theorem, [source](#stmt-na-unitary-states)): Equivalent pure states of a unital $C^*$-algebra $B$ are conjugate by a unitary of $B$. Consequently, if they each have a unique state extension to an algebra $C\supset B$, their extensions are equivalent.
  - Explanation: Equivalent pure states are conjugate by an internal unitary, obtained from Kadison transitivity, so their unique extensions are also equivalent.
- **claim-peaking-restriction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-na-peaking-restriction)): Let $B\subset C$ be a unital inclusion and let $(P_n)$ be a decreasing sequence of projections in $B$ that peaks to a state $H$ of $C$. Then $(P_n)$ peaks to $h=H\|_B$ in $B$, and $H$ is the unique state extension of $h$ to $C$.
  - Explanation: A projection sequence in a subalgebra that peaks to a state of the larger algebra also peaks in the subalgebra, and the state of the larger algebra is the unique extension of the restriction.
- **claim-decreasing-excision** (theorem, [source](#stmt-na-excision)): Let $h$ be a pure state of a unital $C^*$-algebra $B$. There is a decreasing net of positive contractions $x_\lambda\in B$ such that $h(x_\lambda)=1$, $x_\lambda$ decreases strongly to the minimal projection $r_h$ in $B^{**}$, and, for every $a\in B$, $$\label{eq:excision} \\|x_\lambda(a-h(a)1)x_\lambda\\|\longrightarrow0.$$ Moreover, $r_h a r_h=h(a)r_h$ for every $a\in B$.
  - Explanation: For a pure state there is a decreasing net of positive contractions of state value one that decreases to the minimal support projection and compresses every element to its scalar state value in norm.
- **claim-projection-excision** (theorem, [source](#stmt-na-projection-excision)): If decreasing projections $(P_n)$ in $B$ peak to $h$, then $P_n\downarrow r_h$ strongly and $$\\|P_n(a-h(a)1)P_n\\|\longrightarrow0\qquad(a\in B).$$ In particular, if $h(a)=0$, then $\\|P_n a P_t\\|\to0$ on the product index set.
  - Explanation: The excision estimate holds for decreasing projections that peak to the state, and the compressions of an element annihilated by the state vanish on the product index set.
- **claim-cross-excision** (theorem, [source](#stmt-na-cross-excision)): Let $h\not\sim k$ be pure states of $B$, and let $(x_\lambda)$ and $(y_\nu)$ be decreasing nets as in Lemma <a href="#na:excision" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="na:excision">5</a>, for $h$ and $k$ respectively. Then, for every $a\in B$, $$\label{eq:cross-excision} \\|x_\lambda a y_\nu\\|\longrightarrow0$$ on the product directed set. Either net may be a peaking sequence of projections.
  - Explanation: For inequivalent pure states, excision elements for the two states annihilate each other in norm on the product directed set, so inequivalent states are separated in norm.
- **claim-trace-null-support** (theorem, [source](#stmt-na-trace-null)): If $B$ has no finite-dimensional irreducible representations and $\tau$ is a tracial state on $B$, then $\tau(r_h)=0$ for every pure state $h$.
  - Explanation: When the algebra has no finite-dimensional irreducible representations, any tracial state gives zero mass to the minimal support projection of each pure state.
- **claim-trace-expectation-unique** (theorem, [source](#stmt-na-trace-expectation)): For a unital subalgebra $B$ of a faithfully tracial algebra $(C,\tau)$, there is at most one trace-preserving conditional expectation $C\to B$. If one exists, it is faithful.
  - Explanation: Inside a faithfully tracial algebra a trace-preserving conditional expectation onto a unital subalgebra is unique, and whenever it exists it is faithful.
- **claim-peaking-expectation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-na-peaking-expectation)): Let $(B,\tau)$ be faithfully tracial. Suppose decreasing projections $(p_n)_{n\ge0}$ peak to $h$, with $p_0=1$ and trace values strictly decreasing to zero. Then $D=C^*(p_n:n\ge0)$ is isomorphic to $C(\mathbb N_0\cup\{\infty\})$, and there is a trace-preserving conditional expectation $E:B\to D$. Writing $d_n=p_n-p_{n+1}$ for the finite-point atoms, it is given by $$\label{eq:peaking-expectation} E(a)(n)=\frac{\tau(d_nad_n)}{\tau(d_n)},\qquad E(a)(\infty)=h(a).$$
  - Explanation: A peaking projection sequence with strictly decreasing trace values generates a copy of the continuous functions on a convergent sequence, and an explicit trace-preserving conditional expectation onto it exists.
- **claim-simple-extension** (theorem, [source](#stmt-na-simple-extension)): Suppose $B\subset C$ satisfies <a href="#eq:U" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:U">[eq:U]</a>, $B$ is simple, and there is a faithful conditional expectation $E:C\to B$. Then $C$ is simple.
  - Explanation: Unique extension of pure states together with a faithful conditional expectation onto a simple subalgebra forces the larger algebra to be simple.
- **claim-reduced-hnn-extension** (construction, [source](#stmt-na-hnn)): Let $(A,\tau)$ be a unital $C^*$-algebra with faithful tracial state, let $D_1,D_2\subset A$ be unital subalgebras with trace-preserving conditional expectations $E_i:A\to D_i$, and let $\theta:D_1\to D_2$ be a trace-preserving isomorphism. There is a unital extension with a faithful tracial state extending $\tau$, $$H(A)=C^*(A,v),\qquad vdv^*=\theta(d)\quad(d\in D_1),$$ where $v$ is called the *stable unitary*, and a trace-preserving conditional expectation $E_A:H(A)\to A$ annihilating every nonempty reduced word <a href="#na:hnn-word" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="na:hnn-word">[na:hnn-word]</a>. Among faithfully tracial extensions generated by $A$ and such a unitary, the relation and vanishing of the trace on nonempty reduced words determine $H(A)$ uniquely, by an isomorphism fixing $A$ and $v$.
  - Explanation: Given two trace-isomorphic subalgebras with trace-preserving expectations, one can adjoin a stable unitary implementing the isomorphism, obtaining a faithfully tracial extension whose expectation kills reduced words; this extension is unique.
- **claim-free-product-core** (construction, [source](#stmt-na-free-product)): Let $F$ and $G$ be unital $C^*$-algebras with faithful tracial states, containing a common unital subalgebra $D$ on which the traces agree. Suppose that $E_D^F:F\to D$ and $E_D^G:G\to D$ are trace-preserving conditional expectations. There is a unital $C^*$-algebra $Z$ generated by faithful copies of $F$ and $G$ agreeing on $D$, with a faithful tracial state extending both traces and faithful trace-preserving conditional expectations $$E_D:Z\longrightarrow D, \qquad E_F:Z\longrightarrow F, \qquad E_D=E_D^F\circ E_F.$$ The map $E_D$ vanishes on every nonempty alternating product of elements of $\ker E_D^F$ and $\ker E_D^G$. The map $E_F$ vanishes on every product $$\label{na:free-core} a_0 b_1 a_1\cdots b_m a_m, \qquad m\geq1,$$ where $a_0,a_m\in F$, $b_t\in\ker E_D^G$, and $a_t\in\ker E_D^F$ for $0<t<m$. The linear span of $F$ and these products is dense in $Z$.
  - Explanation: Two faithfully tracial algebras sharing a subalgebra with compatible expectations are amalgamated in a reduced free product with a faithful trace and faithful expectations that vanish on alternating centered products.
- **claim-hnn-restriction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-na-hnn-restriction)): With the hypotheses of Proposition <a href="#na:hnn" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="na:hnn">12</a>, let $A'\subset A$ be a unital subalgebra containing $D_1$ and $D_2$. Then $C^*(A',v)\subset H(A)$ is the reduced adjunction $H(A')$ formed with the restricted trace and expectations $E_i\|_{A'}$. The expectation $E_A$ restricts to the expectation $H(A')\to A'$.
  - Explanation: Restricting the reduced adjunction to a subalgebra containing both data algebras gives the reduced adjunction of that subalgebra, compatibly with the expectation.
- **claim-reorder-adjunctions** (theorem, [source](#stmt-na-reorder)): Let $O$ be a unital faithfully tracial $C^*$-algebra. For each $t\in\{j,k\}$, let $D_1^t,D_2^t\subset O$, $E_i^t:O\to D_i^t$ and $\theta_t:D_1^t\to D_2^t$ satisfy the hypotheses of Proposition <a href="#na:hnn" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="na:hnn">12</a>. When forming an adjunction over $H_j(O)$ or $H_k(O)$, extend the other data expectations by composition with the expectation onto $O$. There is a trace-preserving isomorphism $$\label{na:reorder-equation} H_k(H_j(O))\ \cong\ H_j(H_k(O))$$ fixing $O$ and the named stable unitaries $v_j,v_k$.
  - Explanation: Two reduced adjunctions with data in a common base can be performed in either order, up to a trace-preserving isomorphism fixing the base and both stable unitaries.
- **claim-hnn-limit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-na-hnn-limit)): Let $\lambda$ be a nonzero limit ordinal, and let $(O_\beta)_{\beta<\lambda}$ be an increasing family of unital $C^*$-algebras with compatible faithful tracial states. Suppose that fixed subalgebras $D_1,D_2$ lie in every $O_\beta$, with a fixed trace-preserving isomorphism $\theta:D_1\to D_2$ and compatible trace-preserving conditional expectations $E_i^\beta:O_\beta\to D_i$. Use the embeddings of Corollary <a href="#na:hnn-restriction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="na:hnn-restriction">14</a> to identify the reduced adjunctions $H(O_\beta)$ with the same stable unitary $v$. Then, with $$O=\overline{\bigcup_{\beta<\lambda}O_\beta},\qquad R=\overline{\bigcup_{\beta<\lambda}H(O_\beta)},$$ the expectations extend to $O\to D_i$, and $R=H(O)$ with its specified generators and trace.
  - Explanation: Reduced adjunctions commute with norm closures of increasing unions along a limit ordinal when the data are fixed and compatible.
- **claim-bridge** (theorem, [source](#stmt-na-bridge)): Let $A$ be a unital $C^*$-algebra with a faithful tracial state $\tau$ and no finite-dimensional irreducible representations. For $i=1,2$, let $(p_n^i)_{n\geq0}$ be a decreasing sequence of projections, with $p_0^i=1$, peaking to a pure state $f_i$. Suppose that $f_1\not\sim f_2$ and $$\tau(p_n^1)=\tau(p_n^2)=t_n, \qquad 1=t_0>t_1>\cdots>0, \qquad t_n\longrightarrow0.$$ Put $D_i=C^*(p_n^i:n\geq0)$ and let $\theta:D_1\to D_2$ be determined by $\theta(p_n^1)=p_n^2$. Form the reduced HNN extension $H(A)$ of Proposition <a href="#na:hnn" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="na:hnn">12</a> using these data and their trace-preserving conditional expectations. Then every pure state $h$ of $A$ has a unique state extension $\widetilde h$ to $H(A)$. These extensions are pure, and for all pure states $h,k$ of $A$, $$\widetilde h\sim\widetilde k \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad h\sim k \quad\text{or}\quad \{[h],[k]\}=\{[f_1],[f_2]\}.$$ The extension has a faithful tracial state extending $\tau$ and a faithful trace-preserving conditional expectation onto $A$. If $A$ is simple, then $H(A)$ is simple.
  - Explanation: The bridge: the reduced adjunction built from two peaking sequences gives every old pure state a unique pure extension and merges exactly the two designated equivalence classes, while keeping faithful trace, faithful expectation and simplicity.
- **claim-unique-chain** (theorem, [source](#stmt-na-unique-chain)): Let $(B_\alpha)_{\alpha\le\lambda}$ be a continuous chain whose successor inclusions have property <span class="upright">(U)</span>. Then every inclusion $B_\alpha\subset B_\beta$, for $\alpha\le\beta\le\lambda$, has <span class="upright">(U)</span>. The unique lifts of equivalent pure states are equivalent.
  - Explanation: Unique extension of pure states passes through continuous chains whose successor steps have it, and lifts of equivalent pure states stay equivalent.
- **claim-inequivalent-limit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-na-inequivalent-limit)): Let $(B_\alpha)_{\alpha\le\lambda}$ be continuous, with $\lambda$ a nonzero limit ordinal. Suppose pure states $h,k$ of $B_\lambda$ restrict to inequivalent pure states of $B_\alpha$ for all $\alpha$ in some tail below $\lambda$. Then $h\not\sim k$.
  - Explanation: Pure states that restrict to inequivalent states on a tail of a continuous chain stay inequivalent at the limit algebra.
- **claim-base-peak** (theorem, [source](#stmt-na-base-peak)): The algebra $A_0$ is separable, simple and infinite-dimensional, its trace is faithful, and $(p_n)$ peaks to a pure state $f_0$. Moreover, $\tau(p_n)=2^{-n}$. Every unital $C^*$-algebra containing $A_0$ has no finite-dimensional irreducible representations.
  - Explanation: The initial infinite tensor product of matrix algebras is a separable simple faithfully tracial base with a peaking sequence, and any unital algebra containing it has no finite-dimensional irreducible representations.
- **claim-inherited-iteration** (theorem, [source](#stmt-na-inherited)): For every dependency-closed $T\subset\delta$, the algebras $A[T\cap\beta]$, $\beta\le\delta$, form a continuous bridge iteration in the inherited order, with identity steps at indices outside $T$. In particular, $A[T]$ is simple and has a faithful tracial state. If $T$ is countable, $A[T]$ is separable.
  - Explanation: A dependency-closed set of indices inherits a continuous bridge iteration, so the generated subalgebra is simple, faithfully tracial, and separable when the set is countable.
- **claim-closed-extension** (theorem, [source](#stmt-na-closed-extension)): For every dependency-closed $S\subset\delta$, the inclusion $A[S]\subset X_\delta$ has property <span class="upright">(U)</span>.
  - Explanation: Every dependency-closed subalgebra includes into the full iteration with unique extension of pure states, proved by postponing omitted adjunctions to the end.
- **claim-countable-determination** (theorem, [source](#stmt-na-countable-determination)): For every pure state $h$ of $X_\delta$, there is a countable dependency-closed $S\subset\delta$ such that $h\|_{A[S]}$ is pure and $h$ is its unique state extension to $X_\delta$.
  - Explanation: Each pure state of the full algebra is determined by its pure restriction to a separable subalgebra generated by a countable dependency-closed set.
- **claim-pure-state-count** (bound, [source](#stmt-na-state-count)): Put $\mathfrak c=2^{\aleph_0}$. If $\|\delta\|\le\mathfrak c$, then $X_\delta$ has at most $\mathfrak c$ pure states.
  - Explanation: When the index set has size at most the continuum, the algebra has at most continuum many pure states, by counting countable subsets and states on separable algebras.
- **claim-pure-state-homogeneity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-na-homogeneity)): Let $B$ be a separable $C^*$-algebra. If pure states $h,k$ of $B$ have GNS representations with the same kernel, there is an automorphism $\gamma$ of $B$ such that $h\circ\gamma=k$. In particular, this holds for every pair of pure states of a separable simple $C^*$-algebra.
  - Explanation: Pure states of a separable algebra with the same GNS kernel are related by an automorphism; in particular any two pure states of a separable simple algebra are.

{
  "claims": [
    {
      "conclusion": {

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-na-peaking-expectation",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(B,\\tau)$ be faithfully tracial. Suppose decreasing projections $(p_n)_{n\\ge0}$ peak to $h$, with $p_0=1$ and trace values strictly decreasing to zero. Then $D=C^*(p_n:n\\ge0)$ is isomorphic to $C(\\mathbb N_0\\cup\\{\\infty\\})$, and there is a trace-preserving conditional expectation $E:B\\to D$. Writing $d_n=p_n-p_{n+1}$ for the finite-point atoms, it is given by $$\\label{eq:peaking-expectation}\n E(a)(n)=\\frac{\\tau(d_nad_n)}{\\tau(d_n)},\\qquad E(a)(\\infty)=h(a).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-297.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-na-peaking-expectation",
          "exact_quote": "Let $(B,\\tau)$ be faithfully tracial. Suppose decreasing projections $(p_n)_{n\\ge0}$ peak to $h$, with $p_0=1$ and trace values strictly decreasing to zero.           "source_id": "PM-297.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $(B,\\tau)$ be faithfully tracial. Suppose decreasing projections $(p_n)_{n\\ge0}$ peak to $h$, with $p_0=1$ and trace values strictly decreasing to zero.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-na-hnn",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(A,\\tau)$ be a unital $C^*$-algebra with faithful tracial state, let $D_1,D_2\\subset A$ be unital subalgebras with trace-preserving conditional expectations $E_i:A\\to D_i$, and let $\\theta:D_1\\to D_2$ be a trace-preserving isomorphism. There is a unital extension with a faithful tracial state extending $\\tau$, $$H(A)=C^*(A,v),\\qquad vdv^*=\\theta(d)\\quad(d\\in D_1),$$ where $v$ is called the *stable unitary*, and a trace-preserving conditional expectation $E_A:H(A)\\to A$ annihilating every nonempty reduced word <a href=\"#na:hnn-word\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"na:hnn-word\">[na:hnn-word]</a>. Among faithfully tracial extensions generated by $A$ and such a unitary, the relation and vanishing of the trace on nonempty reduced words determine $H(A)$ uniquely, by an isomorphism fixing $A$ and $v$.",
        "source_id": "PM-297.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-na-hnn",
          "exact_quote": "Let $(A,\\tau)$ be a unital $C^*$-algebra with faithful tracial state, let $D_1,D_2\\subset A$ be unital subalgebras with trace-preserving conditional expectations $E_i:A\\to D_i$, and let $\\theta:D_1\\to D_2$ be a trace-preserving isomorphism. There is a unital extension with a faithful tracial state extending $\\tau$, $$H(A)=C^*(A,v),\\qquad vdv^*=\\theta(d)\\quad(d\\in D_1),$$ where $v$ is called the *stable unitary*, and a trace-preserving conditional expectation $E_A:H(A)\\to A$ annihilating every nonempty reduced word <a href=\"#na:hnn-word\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"na:hnn-word\">[na:hnn-word]</a>. Among faithfully tracial extensions generated by $A$ and such a unitary, the relation and vanishing of the trace on nonempty reduced words determine $H(A)$ uniquely, by an isomorphism fixing $A$ and $v$.",
          "source_id": "PM-297.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $(A,\\tau)$ be a unital $C^*$-algebra with faithful tracial state, let $D_1,D_2\\subset A$ be unital subalgebras with trace-preserving conditional expectations $E_i:A\\to D_i$, and let $\\theta:D_1\\to D_2$ be a trace-preserving isomorphism. There is a unital extension with a faithful tracial state extending $\\tau$, $$H(A)=C^*(A,v),\\qquad vdv^*=\\theta(d)\\quad(d\\in D_1),$$ where $v$ is called the *stable unitary*, and a trace-preserving conditional expectation $E_A:H(A)\\to A$ annihilating every nonempty reduced word <a href=\"#na:hnn-word\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"na:hnn-word\">[na:hnn-word]</a>.       "explanation": "Given two trace-isomorphic subalgebras with trace-preserving expectations, one can adjoin a stable unitary implementing the isomorphism, obtaining a faithfully tracial extension whose expectation kills reduced words; this extension is unique.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [

          "source_id": "PM-297.1"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-na-free-product",
        "exact_quote": "Let $F$ and $G$ be unital $C^*$-algebras with faithful tracial states, containing a common unital subalgebra $D$ on which the traces agree. Suppose that $E_D^F:F\\to D$ and $E_D^G:G\\to D$ are trace-preserving conditional expectations. There is a unital $C^*$-algebra $Z$ generated by faithful copies of $F$ and $G$ agreeing on $D$, with a faithful tracial state extending both traces and faithful trace-preserving conditional expectations $$E_D:Z\\longrightarrow D,\n \\qquad E_F:Z\\longrightarrow F,\n \\qquad E_D=E_D^F\\circ E_F.$$ The map $E_D$ vanishes on every nonempty alternating product of elements of $\\ker E_D^F$ and $\\ker E_D^G$. The map $E_F$ vanishes on every product $$\\label{na:free-core}\n a_0 b_1 a_1\\cdots b_m a_m,\n \\qquad m\\geq1,$$ where $a_0,a_m\\in F$, $b_t\\in\\ker E_D^G$, and $a_t\\in\\ker E_D^F$ for $0<t<m$. The linear span of $F$ and these products is dense in $Z$.",
        "source_id": "PM-297.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-na-free-product",
          "exact_quote": "Let $F$ and $G$ be unital $C^*$-algebras with faithful tracial states, containing a common unital subalgebra $D$ on which the traces agree. There is a unital $C^*$-algebra $Z$ generated by faithful copies of $F$ and $G$ agreeing on $D$, with a faithful tracial state extending both traces and faithful trace-preserving conditional expectations $$E_D:Z\\longrightarrow D,\n \\qquad E_F:Z\\longrightarrow F,\n \\qquad E_D=E_D^F\\circ E_F.$$ The map $E_D$ vanishes on every nonempty alternating product of elements of $\\ker E_D^F$ and $\\ker E_D^G$. The map $E_F$ vanishes on every product $$\\label{na:free-core}\n a_0 b_1 a_1\\cdots b_m a_m,\n \\qquad m\\geq1,$$ where $a_0,a_m\\in F$, $b_t\\in\\ker E_D^G$, and $a_t\\in\\ker E_D^F$ for $0<t<m$. The linear span of $F$ and these products is dense in $Z$.",

        "anchor": "stmt-na-hnn-restriction",
        "exact_quote": "With the hypotheses of Proposition <a href=\"#na:hnn\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"na:hnn\">12</a>, let $A'\\subset A$ be a unital subalgebra containing $D_1$ and $D_2$.         "source_id": "PM-297.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

        "anchor": "stmt-na-reorder",
        "exact_quote": "Let $O$ be a unital faithfully tracial $C^*$-algebra. For each $t\\in\\{j,k\\}$, let $D_1^t,D_2^t\\subset O$, $E_i^t:O\\to D_i^t$ and $\\theta_t:D_1^t\\to D_2^t$ satisfy the hypotheses of Proposition <a href=\"#na:hnn\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"na:hnn\">12</a>. When forming an adjunction over $H_j(O)$ or $H_k(O)$, extend the other data expectations by composition with the expectation onto $O$. There is a trace-preserving isomorphism $$\\label{na:reorder-equation}\n H_k(H_j(O))\\ \\cong\\ H_j(H_k(O))$$ fixing $O$ and the named stable unitaries $v_j,v_k$.",
        "source_id": "PM-297.1"
      },

When forming an adjunction over $H_j(O)$ or $H_k(O)$, extend the other data expectations by composition with the expectation onto $O$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-na-hnn-limit",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\lambda$ be a nonzero limit ordinal, and let $(O_\\beta)_{\\beta<\\lambda}$ be an increasing family of unital $C^*$-algebras with compatible faithful tracial states. Suppose that fixed subalgebras $D_1,D_2$ lie in every $O_\\beta$, with a fixed trace-preserving isomorphism $\\theta:D_1\\to D_2$ and compatible trace-preserving conditional expectations $E_i^\\beta:O_\\beta\\to D_i$. Use the embeddings of Corollary <a href=\"#na:hnn-restriction\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"na:hnn-restriction\">14</a> to identify the reduced adjunctions $H(O_\\beta)$ with the same stable unitary $v$. Then, with $$O=\\overline{\\bigcup_{\\beta<\\lambda}O_\\beta},\\qquad\n R=\\overline{\\bigcup_{\\beta<\\lambda}H(O_\\beta)},$$ the expectations extend to $O\\to D_i$, and $R=H(O)$ with its specified generators and trace.",
        "source_id": "PM-297.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-na-hnn-limit",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\lambda$ be a nonzero limit ordinal, and let $(O_\\beta)_{\\beta<\\lambda}$ be an increasing family of unital $C^*$-algebras with compatible faithful tracial states. Use the embeddings of Corollary <a href=\"#na:hnn-restriction\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"na:hnn-restriction\">14</a> to identify the reduced adjunctions $H(O_\\beta)$ with the same stable unitary $v$. Then, with $$O=\\overline{\\bigcup_{\\beta<\\lambda}O_\\beta},\\qquad\n R=\\overline{\\bigcup_{\\beta<\\lambda}H(O_\\beta)},$$ the expectations extend to $O\\to D_i$, and $R=H(O)$ with its specified generators and trace.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-na-bridge",
        "exact_quote": "Let $A$ be a unital $C^*$-algebra with a faithful tracial state $\\tau$ and no finite-dimensional irreducible representations. For $i=1,2$, let $(p_n^i)_{n\\geq0}$ be a decreasing sequence of projections, with $p_0^i=1$, peaking to a pure state $f_i$. Suppose that $f_1\\not\\sim f_2$ and $$\\tau(p_n^1)=\\tau(p_n^2)=t_n,\n \\qquad 1=t_0>t_1>\\cdots>0,\n \\qquad t_n\\longrightarrow0.$$ Put $D_i=C^*(p_n^i:n\\geq0)$ and let $\\theta:D_1\\to D_2$ be determined by $\\theta(p_n^1)=p_n^2$. Form the reduced HNN extension $H(A)$ of Proposition <a href=\"#na:hnn\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"na:hnn\">12</a> using these data and their trace-preserving conditional expectations. Then every pure state $h$ of $A$ has a unique state extension $\\widetilde h$ to $H(A)$. These extensions are pure, and for all pure states $h,k$ of $A$, $$\\widetilde h\\sim\\widetilde k\n \\quad\\Longleftrightarrow\\quad\n h\\sim k\n \\quad\\text{or}\\quad\n \\{[h],[k]\\}=\\{[f_1],[f_2]\\}.$$ The extension has a faithful tracial state extending $\\tau$ and a faithful trace-preserving conditional expectation onto $A$. If $A$ is simple, then $H(A)$ is simple.",
        "source_id": "PM-297.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-na-bridge",

Form the reduced HNN extension $H(A)$ of Proposition <a href=\"#na:hnn\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"na:hnn\">12</a> using these data and their trace-preserving conditional expectations. Then every pure state $h$ of $A$ has a unique state extension $\\widetilde h$ to $H(A)$. These extensions are pure, and for all pure states $h,k$ of $A$, $$\\widetilde h\\sim\\widetilde k\n \\quad\\Longleftrightarrow\\quad\n h\\sim k\n \\quad\\text{or}\\quad\n \\{[h],[k]\\}=\\{[f_1],[f_2]\\}.$$ The extension has a faithful tracial state extending $\\tau$ and a faithful trace-preserving conditional expectation onto $A$.           "source_id": "PM-297.1"

      "explanation": "The bridge: the reduced adjunction built from two peaking sequences gives every old pure state a unique pure extension and merges exactly the two designated equivalence classes, while keeping faithful trace, faithful expectation and simplicity.",

      "conclusion": {

        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-na-inherited",
        "exact_quote": "For every dependency-closed $T\\subset\\delta$, the algebras $A[T\\cap\\beta]$, $\\beta\\le\\delta$, form a continuous bridge iteration in the inherited order, with identity steps at indices outside $T$. In particular, $A[T]$ is simple and has a faithful tracial state. If $T$ is countable, $A[T]$ is separable.",
        "source_id": "PM-297.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **state and pure state** ([source](#sec:states)): A *state* on an algebra $B$ is a positive linear functional of norm one, and it is *pure* if it is an extreme point of the state space.
- **tracial algebra** ([source](#sec:states)): A *tracial algebra* is a unital $C^*$-algebra equipped with a faithful tracial state $\tau$: one has $\tau(ab)=\tau(ba)$, and $\tau(a^*a)=0$ implies $a=0$.
- **support projection** ([source](#pm-h-7)): For a state $h$, its *support projection* $r_h\in B^{**}$ is the least projection on which its normal extension takes value one.
- **conditional expectation** ([source](#pm-h-8)): A *conditional expectation* $E:C\to B$ is a unital completely positive map fixing $B$ and satisfying $E(b_1ab_2)=b_1E(a)b_2$ for $b_1,b_2\in B$.
- **centered element** ([source](#pm-h-11)): An element is *centered* for a conditional expectation $E$ if it belongs to $\ker E$.
- **continuous chain** ([source](#pm-h-15)): A chain $(B_\alpha)_{\alpha\le\lambda}$ of unital inclusions is *continuous* if $B_\gamma=\overline{\bigcup_{\alpha<\gamma}B_\alpha}$ at every nonzero limit ordinal $\gamma\le\lambda$.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-unique-lift** [claim](#stmt-na-unique-lift) / [proof](#proof-stmt-na-unique-lift): Purity forces both parts of a convex decomposition to restrict to the same state, then uniqueness collapses them. -> unique extension is pure
- **step-unitary-states** [claim](#stmt-na-unitary-states) / [proof](#proof-stmt-na-unitary-states): Kadison transitivity applied to a logarithm of a unitary carrying one vector to the other. -> unitary conjugacy of states and of their unique lifts
- **step-peaking-restriction** [claim](#stmt-na-peaking-restriction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-na-peaking-restriction): Extend states of the subalgebra and compare constraints. -> peaking and unique extension for the restriction
- **step-excision** [claim](#stmt-na-excision) / [proof](#proof-stmt-na-excision): Approximate identity of the null algebra of the state, strong limit to a minimal support projection, norming-state argument. -> decreasing excision net
- **step-projection-excision** [claim](#stmt-na-projection-excision) / [proof](#proof-stmt-na-projection-excision): Same norming-state argument for projections, then nesting. -> projection excision estimate
- **step-cross-excision** [claim](#stmt-na-cross-excision) / [proof](#proof-stmt-na-cross-excision): Support projection of one state is annihilated by the other representation; choose indices in sequence. -> product-net norm decay
- **step-trace-null** [claim](#stmt-na-trace-null) / [proof](#proof-stmt-na-trace-null): Orthogonal translates of the support projection by unitaries, equal traces, counting. -> trace of support projection vanishes
- **step-trace-expectation** [claim](#stmt-na-trace-expectation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-na-trace-expectation): Bimodularity plus faithfulness of the trace. -> uniqueness and faithfulness
- **step-peaking-expectation** [claim](#stmt-na-peaking-expectation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-na-peaking-expectation): Define the expectation coordinatewise and use projection excision for continuity. -> explicit trace-preserving expectation onto the abelian algebra
- **step-simple-extension** [claim](#stmt-na-simple-extension) / [proof](#proof-stmt-na-simple-extension): Pure states of the base extend uniquely through a quotient, so they factor through the expectation. -> simplicity of the extension
- **step-free-product** [claim](#stmt-na-free-product) / [proof](#proof-stmt-na-free-product): Hilbert module free-product construction with vacuum compression and projection onto one factor. -> free-product algebra with faithful trace and expectations
- **step-hnn** [claim](#stmt-na-hnn) / [proof](#proof-stmt-na-free-product-2): Matrix corner of an amalgamated free product; uniqueness via trace moments and norm as a limit of moments. -> reduced adjunction with stable unitary
- **step-hnn-restriction** [claim](#stmt-na-hnn-restriction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-na-hnn-restriction): Restrict the data and use the uniqueness clause of the adjunction. -> restricted adjunction identification
- **step-reorder** [claim](#stmt-na-reorder) / [proof](#proof-stmt-na-reorder): Show the two adjunctions are free over the base; the moment recursion is symmetric. -> isomorphism of the two orders
- **step-hnn-limit** [claim](#stmt-na-hnn-limit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-na-hnn-limit): Extend expectations and traces to the closure; approximate reduced words at earlier stages. -> limit of adjunctions is the adjunction of the limit
- **step-bridge** [claim](#stmt-na-bridge) / [proof](#proof-stmt-na-bridge): Pass to the two-by-two free product, prove norm decay of reduced words by excision and cross-excision, deduce unique lifts and exclusion of other joins, return to the corner. -> selective join with unique extensions
- **step-unique-chain** [claim](#stmt-na-unique-chain) / [proof](#proof-stmt-na-unique-chain): Transfinite induction: compose at successors, agree on dense unions at limits. -> unique extension along the chain
- **step-inequivalent-limit** [claim](#stmt-na-inequivalent-limit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-na-inequivalent-limit): Intertwiner between cyclic subspaces vanishes, then continuity contradicts cyclicity. -> inequivalence at the limit
- **step-base-peak** [claim](#stmt-na-base-peak) / [proof](#proof-stmt-na-base-peak): Compatible vector states on the matrix stages; simplicity forbids finite-dimensional quotients. -> properties of the base algebra
- **step-inherited** [claim](#stmt-na-inherited) / [proof](#proof-stmt-na-inherited): Check the bridge hypotheses on the smaller base using restriction results. -> inherited bridge iteration
- **step-closed-extension** [claim](#stmt-na-closed-extension) / [proof](#proof-stmt-na-closed-extension): Insert each omitted index after the retained ones, commute adjunctions, apply the bridge and chain results. -> unique extension from dependency-closed subalgebras
- **step-countable-determination** [claim](#stmt-na-countable-determination) / [proof](#proof-stmt-na-countable-determination): Build countable dependency-closed sets using excision witnesses; show purity of the restriction; use closed extension. -> countable determination of pure states
- **step-state-count** [claim](#stmt-na-state-count) / [proof](#proof-stmt-na-state-count): Count countable subsets and states on separable algebras. -> bound on pure states
- **step-homogeneity** [claim](#stmt-na-homogeneity) / [proof](#stmt-na-homogeneity): Cited theorem of Kishimoto, Ozawa and Sakai. -> automorphism carrying one pure state to the other
- **step-main-recursion** [claim](#stmt-na-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-na-homogeneity): Fixed schedule, transfinite recursion with bridges at scheduled stages, countable determination catches every final pure state. -> algebra with one irreducible representation class

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-main-existence**: A unital infinite-dimensional simple complex C*-algebra with a faithful tracial state exists whose nonzero irreducible representations are all unitarily equivalent, so Naimark's problem has a negative answer in ZFC.
- **inv-unique-lift-pure**: A unique state extension of a pure state across an inclusion is itself pure.
- **inv-bridge-selective-join**: The reduced HNN bridge extends every old pure state uniquely and purely, and merges exactly the two designated equivalence classes and no others.
- **inv-countable-determination**: Every pure state of the iterated algebra is determined, with a unique extension, by its restriction to a separable subalgebra generated by a countable dependency-closed set of steps.
- **inv-state-count-bound**: When the iteration index set has size at most the continuum, the algebra has at most continuum many pure states, which lets a fixed schedule treat all of them.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-separable-quantum-model**: Treat the resulting algebra as a separable model, for example a separably represented system suitable for simulation. -> The algebra is built along a transfinite chain and is not claimed to be separable; the known separable case forces the compact operators, so a separable stand-in destroys the property.
- **anti-finite-dimensional-matrix-reading**: Read the counterexample through finite matrix algebras or the compact operators on a finite-dimensional space. -> Unital compact-operator algebras occur only for finite-dimensional spaces, which are excluded by the infinite-dimensionality requirement, so the comparison with K(H) yields nothing.
- **anti-faithful-separable-representation**: Assume that a faithful representation on a separable Hilbert space gives a usable irreducible model of the algebra. -> Such a representation exists for the alternative construction but is not irreducible, so it does not carry the uniqueness-of-irreducible-representation property.
- **anti-explicit-schedule-run**: Run the recursion as an explicit program that enumerates states and performs the bridge steps. -> The recursion uses well-ordered sets of codes and arbitrary choices over sets of continuum-scale size, so there is no finite procedure to execute.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main result is a pure existence theorem proved by a transfinite recursion over the successor of the continuum with arbitrary set-sized choices, and the algebra produced is not described by any finite procedure; no finite constructive input/output computation exists to implement or test.

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-no-separability** (formal_scope): The counterexample carries no separability assumption. The source notes that a unique irreducible representation on a separable Hilbert space forces the compact operators, so the algebra cannot be separably and irreducibly represented. Blocked use: ['Treating the algebra as separable or as having a separable irreducible representation space', 'Using it as a concrete separable model']
- **limit-bridge-base-hypotheses** (hypothesis): The bridge theorem needs a base with a faithful tracial state and no finite-dimensional irreducible representations. Without these, the support projections of pure states need not be trace-null and the trace-based excision arguments fail. Blocked use: ['Applying the bridge to bases that have finite-dimensional irreducible representations', 'Applying the bridge to bases lacking a faithful trace']
- **limit-bridge-trace-sequences** (hypothesis): The two peaking sequences must start at the unit, must be peaking to inequivalent pure states, and must have equal trace values strictly decreasing to zero. Only then does the abelian data algebra carry a trace-preserving expectation and the isomorphism exchanging the sequences preserve the trace. Blocked use: ['Joining states whose peaking sequences have different or non-strictly-decreasing trace values', 'Joining states that are already equivalent']
- **limit-dependency-closed-sets** (hypothesis): Unique extension and countable determination are stated only for dependency-closed index sets. An arbitrary subset of steps need not give a subalgebra with unique extension into the whole iteration. Blocked use: ['Restricting to arbitrary countable sets of bridge steps and still claiming unique extension', 'Dropping the dependency closure when choosing a determining subalgebra']
- **limit-homogeneity-separable** (hypothesis): The homogeneity theorem is applied only to separable algebras, here the determining subalgebras. It gives no automorphism carrying one pure state to another on the full non-separable algebra. Blocked use: ['Applying homogeneity directly to the full iterated algebra', 'Applying homogeneity to pure states whose GNS kernels differ']
- **limit-state-count-index-size** (edge_case): The bound on the number of pure states needs the index set to have size at most the continuum. At the final length the bound is not used for the final algebra itself; it is used only at earlier stages. Blocked use: ['Using the continuum bound for iterations longer than the continuum', 'Concluding the final algebra has few pure states from this bound']
- **limit-nonconstructive-recursion** (nonconstructive): The main construction is a transfinite recursion with set-sized choices; no finite procedure produces the algebra, its states, or the schedule. Blocked use: ['Implementing the recursion as a program', 'Extracting an explicit description or complexity bound from the existence proof']
- **limit-length-not-density** (formal_scope): The construction has a stated length, but the source explicitly makes no claim that this length equals the density of the resulting algebra. Blocked use: ['Reading the iteration length as the density or cardinality of the algebra']
- **limit-faithful-representation-not-irreducible** (counterexample_scope): A faithful representation on a separable Hilbert space, as in the alternative construction mentioned in the source, is not irreducible, so it says nothing about uniqueness of irreducible representations. Blocked use: ['Inferring the uniqueness property from the existence of a faithful separable representation']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 297 (`lean/docs/297.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [A counterexample to Naimark's problem in ZFC](../../preprints/A-counterexample-to-Naimarks-problem-in-ZFC-September-24-2026/naimark-counterexample-zfc.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> Naimark's problem asks whether a $C^*$-algebra whose nonzero irreducible representations are all unitarily equivalent must be an algebra of compact operators. The formalization gives a counterexample in ZFC: a unital infinite-dimensional simple complex $C^*$-algebra with a faithful tracial state has that uniqueness property for irreducible representations but is not isomorphic to the compact operators on any Hilbert space. No additional set-theoretic assumption is used.

| `IsSimpleCStar` | def |
| `IsState` | def |
| `IsFaithfulTracialState` | def |
| `IsIrreducible` | def |
| `UnitarilyEquivalent` | def |
| `HasUniqueIrreducibleRepresentation` | def |
| `IsIsomorphicToCompacts` | def |
| `main` | theorem |

Comparator source `Naimark.lean` (2438 bytes, sha256 `68b364f0998bd30cd0ebf8c87c591267df29b97f183204e93396bda7befe86e0`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

noncomputable section

universe u v w

namespace NaimarkZFC

def IsSimpleCStar (A : Type u) [CStarAlgebra A] : Prop :=
  Nontrivial A ∧ ∀ I : TwoSidedIdeal A, IsClosed (I : Set A) → I = ⊥ ∨ I = ⊤

def IsState {A : Type u} [CStarAlgebra A] (f : A →L[ℂ] ℂ) : Prop :=
  ‖f‖ = 1 ∧ ∀ a : A, (f (star a * a)).im = 0 ∧ 0 ≤ (f (star a * a)).re

def IsFaithfulTracialState {A : Type u} [CStarAlgebra A] (f : A →L[ℂ] ℂ) : Prop :=
  IsState f ∧ (∀ a b : A, f (a * b) = f (b * a)) ∧
    ∀ a : A, a ≠ 0 → 0 < (f (star a * a)).re

def IsIrreducible {A : Type u} [CStarAlgebra A]
    {H : Type v} [NormedAddCommGroup H] [InnerProductSpace ℂ H] [CompleteSpace H]
    (π : A →⋆ₙₐ[ℂ] (H →L[ℂ] H)) : Prop :=
  π ≠ 0 ∧ ∀ M : Submodule ℂ H, IsClosed (M : Set H) →
    (∀ a : A, ∀ x ∈ M, π a x ∈ M) → M = ⊥ ∨ M = ⊤

def UnitarilyEquivalent {A : Type u} [CStarAlgebra A]
    {H : Type v} [NormedAddCommGroup H] [InnerProductSpace ℂ H] [CompleteSpace H]
    {K : Type w} [NormedAddCommGroup K] [InnerProductSpace ℂ K] [CompleteSpace K]
    (π : A →⋆ₙₐ[ℂ] (H →L[ℂ] H)) (ρ : A →⋆ₙₐ[ℂ] (K →L[ℂ] K)) : Prop :=
  ∃ U : H ≃ₗᵢ[ℂ] K, ∀ a : A, ∀ x : H, U (π a x) = ρ a (U x)

def HasUniqueIrreducibleRepresentation (A : Type u) [CStarAlgebra A] : Prop :=
  ∀ (H : Type v) [NormedAddCommGroup H] [InnerProductSpace ℂ H] [CompleteSpace H]
    (K : Type w) [NormedAddCommGroup K] [InnerProductSpace ℂ K] [CompleteSpace K]
    (π : A →⋆ₙₐ[ℂ] (H →L[ℂ] H)) (ρ : A →⋆ₙₐ[ℂ] (K →L[ℂ] K)),
    IsIrreducible π → IsIrreducible ρ → UnitarilyEquivalent π ρ

def IsIsomorphicToCompacts (A : Type u) [CStarAlgebra A]
    (H : Type v) [NormedAddCommGroup H] [InnerProductSpace ℂ H] [CompleteSpace H] : Prop :=
  ∃ φ : A →⋆ₙₐ[ℂ] (H →L[ℂ] H), Function.Injective φ ∧
    ∀ T : H →L[ℂ] H, (∃ a : A, φ a = T) ↔ IsCompactOperator T

theorem main :
    ∃ (A : Type u) (_ : CStarAlgebra A),
      IsSimpleCStar A ∧ ¬ FiniteDimensional ℂ A ∧
      (∃ τ : A →L[ℂ] ℂ, IsFaithfulTracialState τ) ∧
      HasUniqueIrreducibleRepresentation.{u, v, w} A ∧
      ∀ (H : Type v) [NormedAddCommGroup H] [InnerProductSpace ℂ H] [CompleteSpace H],
        ¬ IsIsomorphicToCompacts A H := by
  sorry

end NaimarkZFC

end

end OAI
```

The formalization gives a counterexample in ZFC: a unital infinite-dimensional simple complex $C^*$-algebra with a faithful tracial state has that uniqueness property for irreducible representations but is not isomorphic to the compact operators on any Hilbert space. <a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We construct in ZFC a unital infinite-dimensional simple complex <i>C</i><sup>∗</sup>-algebra with a faithful tracial state whose nonzero irreducible representations are all unitarily equivalent. This gives a negative answer to Naimark's problem without additional set-theoretic assumptions.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

For a complex Hilbert space $H$, let $K(H)$ denote the $C^*$-algebra of compact operators on $H$. Every nonzero irreducible representation of $K(H)$ is unitarily equivalent to its defining representation. Naimark’s problem asks whether this property characterizes the algebras $K(H)$. We give a negative answer in ZFC.

<a id="stmt-na-main"></a>
<div id="na:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *There exists a unital infinite-dimensional simple complex $C^*$-algebra $A$ with a faithful tracial state such that all its nonzero irreducible representations are unitarily equivalent. In particular, $A$ is not isomorphic to $K(H)$ for any complex Hilbert space $H$.*

</div>

Here a representation is irreducible if it is nonzero and has no nontrivial closed invariant subspace. A tracial state $\tau$ is faithful if $\tau(a^*a)>0$ whenever $a\ne0$. The last assertion of the theorem follows from the first: $K(H)$ is unital only when $H$ is finite-dimensional. All algebras and Hilbert spaces in the proof are complex, and no separability assumption is placed on $A$.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### History and scope

Naimark’s question dates to 1951; see the historical account of the question and Rosenberg’s affirmative work in (C. Akemann and Weaver 2004) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[AkemannWeaver2004](#ref-AkemannWeaver2004)]. When the unique nonzero irreducible representation acts on a separable Hilbert space, the algebra is isomorphic to the compact operators; see (Calderón and Farah 2023, Proposition 7.2) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[CalderonFarah2022](#ref-CalderonFarah2022)]. This condition concerns an irreducible representation, not merely a faithful representation on a separable Hilbert space. Akemann and Weaver constructed a counterexample assuming Jensen’s diamond principle (C. Akemann and Weaver 2004, Theorem 5) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[AkemannWeaver2004](#ref-AkemannWeaver2004)]. They also established independence for the existence of a counterexample generated by $\aleph_1$ elements (C. Akemann and Weaver 2004, Corollary 7) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[AkemannWeaver2004](#ref-AkemannWeaver2004)]. The unrestricted existence question does not impose this bound on the number of generators.

Subsequent work refined the set-theoretic hypotheses and the possible structure of counterexamples. Calderón and Farah obtain a counterexample from $\diamondsuit_{\mathrm{Cohen}}$ together with CH (Calderón and Farah 2023, Theorem 5.4) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[CalderonFarah2022](#ref-CalderonFarah2022)]. Under diamond, Vaccaro constructs counterexamples whose tracial state spaces are affinely homeomorphic to any prescribed metrizable Choquet simplex (Vaccaro 2018, 2020) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Vaccaro2018](#ref-Vaccaro2018), [Vaccaro2020](#ref-Vaccaro2020)]. Thus the presence of a trace is already compatible with the earlier constructions.

Tanaka gives another ZFC construction with a faithful tracial state (Tanaka 2026, Theorems 1.1, 4.1 and 5.3) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Tanaka2026](#ref-Tanaka2026)], so the existence conclusion of Theorem <a href="#na:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="na:main">1</a> is already present there. His construction starts from the CAR algebra and uses strong shell sums and rank-one defects. It also gives a faithful representation on a separable Hilbert space; that representation is not irreducible. Here we give an alternative proof organized around a selective bridge for faithfully tracial bases without finite-dimensional irreducible representations, reduced-HNN reordering, and dependency-closed unique extension and countable determination. The precise hypotheses and conclusions of these intermediate results are stated below. Our construction has length $\mathfrak c^+$, where $\mathfrak c=2^{\aleph_0}$; we make no assertion that this length is the density of the resulting algebra.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### The proof in outline

We work with pure states, whose Gelfand–Naimark–Segal (GNS) representations are irreducible. Two pure states are called equivalent when their GNS representations are unitarily equivalent. The construction repeatedly joins a chosen pair of equivalence classes. For this procedure to be controllable, each old pure state must have a unique state extension, and no other pair of old classes may become equivalent.

The main analytic step starts with a faithfully tracial algebra having no finite-dimensional irreducible representations. It adjoins a unitary carrying one decreasing projection sequence to another. Each sequence specifies its state as the only state taking value one on every projection; we call it a *peaking sequence*. When the two states are inequivalent and the sequences have matching trace values strictly decreasing to zero, the resulting reduced HNN extension has exactly the required pure-state properties (Theorem <a href="#na:bridge" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="na:bridge">17</a>). The extension is built from a reduced amalgamated free product using Ueda’s matrix-corner construction (Ueda 2008a, 2008b) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[UedaPreprint2008](#ref-UedaPreprint2008), [Ueda2008](#ref-Ueda2008)]. Pure-state excision (C. A. Akemann, Anderson, and Pedersen 1986) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[AkemannAndersonPedersen1986](#ref-AkemannAndersonPedersen1986)] and the cross-excision estimate of Akemann and Weaver (C. Akemann and Weaver 2004, Lemma 1) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[AkemannWeaver2004](#ref-AkemannWeaver2004)] turn uniqueness of extension and the exclusion of unintended joins into norm-decay estimates for reduced words. The estimates apply to every old pure state.

The second step makes every pure state accessible to the recursion. We start with a separable simple algebra, adjoin one unitary at each active successor stage, and take norm closures of unions at limits. Each adjunction depends on only countably many earlier generators. If a set of generators contains all these dependencies, we prove that every pure state on the algebra it generates has a unique extension to the whole algebra (Theorem <a href="#na:closed-extension" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="na:closed-extension">22</a>). To prove this, we postpone an omitted adjunction past the retained ones. The no-other-join assertion of the bridge keeps its two states inequivalent until the postponed adjunction is performed. For every pure state of the full algebra, excision provides a separable subalgebra generated by a countable dependency-closed set of steps on which the restriction is pure. The closed-subiteration theorem then makes the original state its unique extension to the full algebra (Lemma <a href="#na:countable-determination" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="na:countable-determination">23</a>).

This last conclusion replaces the state-guessing role of a set-theoretic prediction principle. At every stage below $\mathfrak c^+$ there are at most $\mathfrak c$ pure states, so a fixed schedule can eventually treat them all. On each separable simple determining subalgebra, the homogeneity theorem of Kishimoto, Ozawa and Sakai (Kishimoto, Ozawa, and Sakai 2003) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[KishimotoOzawaSakai2003](#ref-KishimotoOzawaSakai2003)] supplies the next peaking sequence. A final countable-determination argument catches every pure state of the limit algebra.

The ingredients have several useful precedents. Akemann and Weaver combine pure-state homogeneity with extensions joining prescribed states (C. Akemann and Weaver 2004, Corollary 3) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[AkemannWeaver2004](#ref-AkemannWeaver2004)]. Farah and Hirshberg realize prescribed countable equivalence patterns with unique pure-state extensions (Farah and Hirshberg 2017, Lemma 2.3) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[FarahHirshberg2017](#ref-FarahHirshberg2017)]; Vaccaro’s corrected construction combines this method with control of the trace space (Vaccaro 2018, 2020) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[Vaccaro2018](#ref-Vaccaro2018), [Vaccaro2020](#ref-Vaccaro2020)]. Akemann, Wassermann and Weaver prove pure-state restriction and unique-extension results for reduced free group algebras (C. Akemann, Wassermann, and Weaver 2010, Proposition 2.3 and Theorem 2.6) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[AkemannWassermannWeaver2010](#ref-AkemannWassermannWeaver2010)]. Calderón and Farah propose using separable subalgebras that uniquely determine the relevant states, followed by an amalgamation preserving unique extension (Calderón and Farah 2023, sec. 8, especially Question 8.1) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[CalderonFarah2022](#ref-CalderonFarah2022)]. The two features established here are the selective bridge for all old pure states and its compatibility with dependency-closed subiterations. Together they give the countable determination needed for the unrestricted recursion.

Section <a href="#sec:states" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:states">2</a> proves the state-theoretic facts used below. Section <a href="#sec:hnn" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:hnn">3</a> constructs the reduced adjunction, and Section <a href="#sec:bridge" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:bridge">4</a> proves its pure-state properties. Section <a href="#sec:iteration" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:iteration">5</a> establishes countable determination; Section <a href="#sec:construction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:construction">6</a> carries out the final recursion.

<a id="sec:states"></a>
### Pure states, excision, and peaking projections

The construction must preserve unique state extensions while changing selected equivalence classes. This section records the state-theoretic tools for doing so. Its main analytic fact is excision: near the support of a pure state, compression of an element approaches its scalar state value.

All algebras and Hilbert spaces are complex. Unless stated otherwise, algebras are unital and inclusions preserve the unit. A *state* on an algebra $B$ is a positive linear functional of norm one, and it is *pure* if it is an extreme point of the state space. Write $(\pi_h,H_h,\xi_h)$ for the GNS representation of a state $h$. Thus $\xi_h$ is a cyclic unit vector and $h(a)=\langle\pi_h(a)\xi_h,\xi_h\rangle$; purity is equivalent to irreducibility of $\pi_h$. For pure states, write $h\sim k$ when $\pi_h$ and $\pi_k$ are unitarily equivalent. We use $\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}(u)(a)=uau^*$ for conjugation by a unitary.

A *tracial algebra* is a unital $C^*$-algebra equipped with a faithful tracial state $\tau$: one has $\tau(ab)=\tau(ba)$, and $\tau(a^*a)=0$ implies $a=0$. Traces and other bounded functionals are extended normally to the enveloping von Neumann algebra $B^{**}$, with the same notation. The normal extension of a trace is tracial, by separate ultraweak continuity. For a representation $\pi$ of $B$, its normal extension to $B^{**}$ is denoted by $\pi^{**}$.

<a id="eq:U"></a>
For an inclusion $B\subset C$, we shall use the property $$\tag{U}\label{eq:U}
 \text{every pure state of $B$ has exactly one state extension to $C$.}$$ Here uniqueness is among all states. States always extend across a unital inclusion, by the positive form of the Hahn–Banach theorem.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### Unique extensions and peaking

<a id="stmt-na-unique-lift"></a>
<div id="na:unique-lift" class="lemma">

**Lemma 2**. *If a pure state $h$ of $B$ has a unique state extension $\widetilde h$ to $C\supset B$, then $\widetilde h$ is pure.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-na-unique-lift"><summary>Proof of stmt-na-unique-lift</summary>

*Proof.* In a nontrivial convex decomposition of $\widetilde h$, both restrictions to $B$ equal $h$ by purity. Uniqueness then makes both states equal to $\widetilde h$. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-na-unitary-states"></a>
<div id="na:unitary-states" class="lemma">

**Lemma 3**. *Equivalent pure states of a unital $C^*$-algebra $B$ are conjugate by a unitary of $B$. Consequently, if they each have a unique state extension to an algebra $C\supset B$, their extensions are equivalent.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-na-unitary-states"><summary>Proof of stmt-na-unitary-states</summary>

*Proof.* Realize the two states by unit vectors $\xi,\eta$ in a common irreducible representation $\pi$. On their finite-dimensional span choose a unitary carrying $\xi$ to $\eta$ and a self-adjoint logarithm of that unitary. Kadison transitivity, including its self-adjoint form (Kadison 1957, Theorem 1) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[Kadison1957](#ref-Kadison1957)], supplies a self-adjoint $a\in B$ whose action on this span is the chosen logarithm. The span reduces $\pi(a)$, so $u=e^{ia}$ satisfies $\pi(u)\xi=\eta$. If $h$ is represented by $\xi$ and $k$ by $\eta$, then $k=h\circ\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}(u^*)$. Conjugating their unique extensions by the same unitary proves the last assertion. ◻

</details>

A decreasing sequence of projections $(P_n)_{n\ge0}$ in $B$ *peaks to* a state $h$ if $h$ is the only state satisfying $h(P_n)=1$ for every $n$. Such a state is pure: both states in any convex decomposition satisfy the same constraints. The following elementary observation will let us pass to smaller algebras without assuming <a href="#eq:U" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:U">[eq:U]</a> for the inclusion.

<a id="stmt-na-peaking-restriction"></a>
<div id="na:peaking-restriction" class="lemma">

**Lemma 4** (Peaking under restriction). *Let $B\subset C$ be a unital inclusion and let $(P_n)$ be a decreasing sequence of projections in $B$ that peaks to a state $H$ of $C$. Then $(P_n)$ peaks to $h=H|_B$ in $B$, and $H$ is the unique state extension of $h$ to $C$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-na-peaking-restriction"><summary>Proof of stmt-na-peaking-restriction</summary>

*Proof.* Every state of $B$ satisfying the projection constraints extends to a state of $C$ with the same constraints, hence to $H$. It must therefore equal $h$. Every extension of $h$ also satisfies the constraints and must equal $H$. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### Excision and inequivalent states

For a state $h$, its *support projection* $r_h\in B^{**}$ is the least projection on which its normal extension takes value one. We shall prove that this projection is minimal when $h$ is pure, together with the norm approximation that we need. The decreasing-net form of pure-state excision used here is proved by Akemann, Anderson, and Pedersen (C. A. Akemann, Anderson, and Pedersen 1986, Proposition 2.2) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[AkemannAndersonPedersen1986](#ref-AkemannAndersonPedersen1986)]. We record a proof in the net form used below.

<a id="stmt-na-excision"></a>
<div id="na:excision" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:excision"></a>
**Lemma 5** (Decreasing excision). *Let $h$ be a pure state of a unital $C^*$-algebra $B$. There is a decreasing net of positive contractions $x_\lambda\in B$ such that $h(x_\lambda)=1$, $x_\lambda$ decreases strongly to the minimal projection $r_h$ in $B^{**}$, and, for every $a\in B$, $$\label{eq:excision}
 \|x_\lambda(a-h(a)1)x_\lambda\|\longrightarrow0.$$ Moreover, $r_h a r_h=h(a)r_h$ for every $a\in B$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-na-excision"><summary>Proof of stmt-na-excision</summary>

*Proof.* Put $$L=\{a\in B:h(a^*a)=0\},\qquad N=L\cap L^*.$$ Here $L$ is a closed left ideal and $N$ is a $C^*$-subalgebra. If $a\in L$, then $a^*a\in N$, since $h((a^*a)^2)\le\|a\|^2h(a^*a)=0$.

First, $h$ is the only state annihilating $N$. If another state $k$ annihilates $N$, then $L$ is contained in the kernel of $a\mapsto\pi_k(a)\xi_k$. The map $Q_h:B\to H_h$, $Q_h(a)=\pi_h(a)\xi_h$, is onto by one-vector Kadison transitivity. This assertion concerns the whole Hilbert space, even when it is nonseparable. Since $\ker Q_h=L$, the open mapping theorem for $B/L\to H_h$ shows that $$T\bigl(\pi_h(a)\xi_h\bigr)=\pi_k(a)\xi_k$$ defines a bounded operator $T:H_h\to H_k$. It intertwines the representations. Thus $T^*T$ is scalar by irreducibility of $\pi_h$; its value on $\xi_h$ shows that $T^*T=1$. Taking vector coefficients gives $k=h$.

We next construct the decreasing net. Order $N_+$ by the usual operator order, a directed order, and set $$e_s=1-(1+s)^{-1},\qquad x_s=1-e_s \qquad(s\in N_+).$$ Functional calculus gives $e_s\in N$, and operator monotonicity of inversion makes $(e_s)$ increasing. For $b\in N$ and $s\ge m(bb^*+b^*b)$, $$\|(1-e_s)b\|^2
 \le \frac1m\|(1+s)^{-1}s(1+s)^{-1}\|
 \le\frac1{4m};$$ the same estimate holds for $\|b(1-e_s)\|^2$. Thus $(e_s)$ is an approximate identity of $N$, and $h(x_s)=1$.

Let $q$ be the strong limit of $(e_s)$, the support projection of $N$. Every normal state supported on $1-q$ annihilates $N$, and hence is $h$; conversely, $h(1-q)=1$. The corner $(1-q)B^{**}(1-q)$ therefore has exactly one normal state. Normal states separate its self-adjoint elements, so this corner is scalar. It follows that $1-q=r_h$ is minimal and $x_s\downarrow r_h$. The identity $r_har_h=h(a)r_h$ follows by applying $h$ to this scalar corner.

Finally, let $\rho_s$ be arbitrary states of $B$. Every weak-star cluster point of the positive functionals $a\mapsto\rho_s(x_sax_s)$ annihilates $N$, by the approximate-identity estimates. A nonzero cluster point, after normalization, is therefore $h$. For self-adjoint $a$, a failure of <a href="#eq:excision" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:excision">[eq:excision]</a> would give a subnet and norming states $\rho_s$ with a cluster point nonzero on $a-h(a)1$, a contradiction. Real and imaginary parts give the conclusion for arbitrary $a$. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-na-projection-excision"></a>
<div id="na:projection-excision" class="lemma">

**Lemma 6** (Projection excision). *If decreasing projections $(P_n)$ in $B$ peak to $h$, then $P_n\downarrow r_h$ strongly and $$\|P_n(a-h(a)1)P_n\|\longrightarrow0\qquad(a\in B).$$ In particular, if $h(a)=0$, then $\|P_n a P_t\|\to0$ on the product index set.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-na-projection-excision"><summary>Proof of stmt-na-projection-excision</summary>

*Proof.* The infimum of the $P_n$ in $B^{**}$ supports exactly one normal state, namely $h$. As in the preceding proof, it is the minimal projection $r_h$. For arbitrary states $\rho_n$, every cluster point $\psi$ of $\rho_n(P_n\,\cdot\,P_n)$ vanishes on $1-P_m$ for each fixed $m$. If $\psi\ne0$, its normalization satisfies every peaking constraint and therefore equals $h$. The same norming-state argument proves excision. For the last assertion use nesting: $$\|P_n a P_t\|\le \|P_{\min(n,t)}aP_{\min(n,t)}\|.
\qedhere$$ ◻

</details>

Excision also separates inequivalent states in norm. We use the following form of the cross-excision estimate of Akemann and Weaver (C. Akemann and Weaver 2004, Lemma 1) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[AkemannWeaver2004](#ref-AkemannWeaver2004)].

<a id="stmt-na-cross-excision"></a>
<div id="na:cross-excision" class="lemma">

**Lemma 7** (Cross-excision). *Let $h\not\sim k$ be pure states of $B$, and let $(x_\lambda)$ and $(y_\nu)$ be decreasing nets as in Lemma <a href="#na:excision" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="na:excision">5</a>, for $h$ and $k$ respectively. Then, for every $a\in B$, <a id="eq:cross-excision"></a>$$\label{eq:cross-excision}
 \|x_\lambda a y_\nu\|\longrightarrow0$$ on the product directed set. Either net may be a peaking sequence of projections.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-na-cross-excision"><summary>Proof of stmt-na-cross-excision</summary>

*Proof.* The normal extension of $\pi_h$ annihilates $r_k$. Otherwise a unit vector in the range of $\pi_h^{**}(r_k)$ would represent $k$, by $r_kar_k=k(a)r_k$. Every nonzero vector in an irreducible representation is cyclic, so this would imply $h\sim k$.

Consequently $h(ay_{\nu_0}a^*)\to0$ as $\nu_0$ increases. For $\nu\ge\nu_0$, the inequalities $y_\nu^2\le y_\nu\le y_{\nu_0}$ give $$\begin{aligned}
 \|x_\lambda a y_\nu\|^2
 &\le \|x_\lambda a y_{\nu_0}a^*x_\lambda\|\\
 &\le h(ay_{\nu_0}a^*)
   +\|x_\lambda(ay_{\nu_0}a^*-h(ay_{\nu_0}a^*)1)x_\lambda\|.
\end{aligned}$$ Choose $\nu_0$ first, then use excision to choose $\lambda_0$. This proves smallness on a rectangular tail and hence the product-net limit. Lemma <a href="#na:projection-excision" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="na:projection-excision">6</a> gives the stated projection case. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-na-trace-null"></a>
<div id="na:trace-null" class="lemma">

**Lemma 8** (Trace-null supports). *If $B$ has no finite-dimensional irreducible representations and $\tau$ is a tracial state on $B$, then $\tau(r_h)=0$ for every pure state $h$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-na-trace-null"><summary>Proof of stmt-na-trace-null</summary>

*Proof.* The space $H_h$ is infinite-dimensional. Choose orthonormal unit vectors $\eta_j$ and unitaries $u_j\in B$ with $\pi_h(u_j)\xi_h=\eta_j$, as in Lemma <a href="#na:unitary-states" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="na:unitary-states">3</a>. The projections $u_jr_hu_j^*$ are pairwise orthogonal, since $$r_hu_i^*u_jr_h=h(u_i^*u_j)r_h=0\qquad(i\ne j).$$ They all have trace $\tau(r_h)$, so $m\tau(r_h)\le1$ for every positive integer $m$. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### Expectations from peaking projections

We now use projection excision to construct the conditional expectations needed for the tracial extensions. A *conditional expectation* $E:C\to B$ is a unital completely positive map fixing $B$ and satisfying $E(b_1ab_2)=b_1E(a)b_2$ for $b_1,b_2\in B$. It is *faithful* if $E(a^*a)=0$ implies $a=0$.

<a id="stmt-na-trace-expectation"></a>
<div id="na:trace-expectation" class="lemma">

**Lemma 9**. *For a unital subalgebra $B$ of a faithfully tracial algebra $(C,\tau)$, there is at most one trace-preserving conditional expectation $C\to B$. If one exists, it is faithful.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-na-trace-expectation"><summary>Proof of stmt-na-trace-expectation</summary>

*Proof.* For such an expectation, bimodularity gives $\tau(b^*E(a))=\tau(b^*a)$ for every $b\in B$. Two expectations therefore have difference $d\in B$ satisfying $\tau(d^*d)=0$, so they agree. Faithfulness follows from $\tau(E(a^*a))=\tau(a^*a)$. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-na-peaking-expectation"></a>
<div id="na:peaking-expectation" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:peaking-expectation"></a>
**Lemma 10**. *Let $(B,\tau)$ be faithfully tracial. Suppose decreasing projections $(p_n)_{n\ge0}$ peak to $h$, with $p_0=1$ and trace values strictly decreasing to zero. Then $D=C^*(p_n:n\ge0)$ is isomorphic to $C(\mathbb N_0\cup\{\infty\})$, and there is a trace-preserving conditional expectation $E:B\to D$. Writing $d_n=p_n-p_{n+1}$ for the finite-point atoms, it is given by $$\label{eq:peaking-expectation}
 E(a)(n)=\frac{\tau(d_nad_n)}{\tau(d_n)},\qquad E(a)(\infty)=h(a).$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-na-peaking-expectation"><summary>Proof of stmt-na-peaking-expectation</summary>

*Proof.* The $d_n$ are nonzero orthogonal projections. Their closed linear span is isomorphic to $c_0(\mathbb N_0)$, and adjoining the unit gives the claimed description of $D$. Indeed $p_n=1-\sum_{j<n}d_j$ and $\|p_n\|=1$, since $\tau(p_n)>0$. Multiplying by $p_n$ shows that $1$ has distance at least one from every linear combination of $d_0,\ldots,d_{n-1}$.

The values in <a href="#eq:peaking-expectation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:peaking-expectation">[eq:peaking-expectation]</a> define a continuous function: by $d_n\le p_n$ and Lemma <a href="#na:projection-excision" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="na:projection-excision">6</a>, $$\left|\frac{\tau(d_nad_n)}{\tau(d_n)}-h(a)\right|
 \le \|p_n(a-h(a)1)p_n\|\longrightarrow0.$$ Every coordinate of $E$ is a state, so $E$ is unital and completely positive: positivity of each matrix amplification is tested pointwise in the commutative range. The map fixes $D$. To check bimodularity, an element of $D$ acts scalarly on each $d_n$; at infinity, $h|_D$ is the evaluation character. Cauchy–Schwarz for $h$ then gives $h(ba)=h(b)h(a)$ and $h(ab)=h(a)h(b)$ for $b\in D$. These coordinate identities prove bimodularity.

Finally, $\sum_n\tau(d_n)=1$ and the trace on $D$ has no mass at infinity. Summing the finite coordinates gives $\tau(E(a))=\tau(a)$, since the remaining trace satisfies $|\tau(p_Na)|\le\|a\|\tau(p_N)\to0$. ◻

</details>

Unique state extensions and a faithful expectation will also preserve simplicity, without a separate analysis of ideals in each extension.

<a id="stmt-na-simple-extension"></a>
<div id="na:simple-extension" class="lemma">

**Lemma 11**. *Suppose $B\subset C$ satisfies <a href="#eq:U" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:U">[eq:U]</a>, $B$ is simple, and there is a faithful conditional expectation $E:C\to B$. Then $C$ is simple.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-na-simple-extension"><summary>Proof of stmt-na-simple-extension</summary>

*Proof.* Let $J$ be a proper closed two-sided ideal of $C$. The quotient map $q:C\to C/J$ is injective on $B$, since $B$ is simple and $q(1)\ne0$. Every pure state $h$ of $B$ therefore extends through this quotient to a state of $C$ annihilating $J$. By <a href="#eq:U" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:U">[eq:U]</a>, this extension is $h\circ E$. Thus $h(E(a))=0$ for every positive $a\in J$ and every pure $h$ on $B$. Pure states separate positive elements, so $E(a)=0$; faithfulness gives $a=0$. Hence $J=0$. ◻

</details>

<a id="sec:hnn"></a>
### Reduced HNN adjunctions

We now construct the extension that will join two pure-state classes. The construction itself has a more general input: two trace-preserving isomorphic subalgebras of a faithfully tracial algebra. It adjoins a unitary implementing the isomorphism, while prescribing the expectation of every word in that unitary. These prescriptions will also let us restrict the construction to smaller bases and interchange two adjunctions with data in the same base.

The matrix-corner construction below is the reduced HNN construction of Ueda; see (Ueda 2008a, Proposition 2.2 and Remark 2.3) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[UedaPreprint2008](#ref-UedaPreprint2008)] and (Ueda 2008b) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[Ueda2008](#ref-Ueda2008)]. We give the details, including the faithful representations and expectations needed later. The corresponding expectation and embedding results for reduced amalgamated free products appear in (Blanchard and Dykema 2001, Lemma 1.1 and Theorem 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[BlanchardDykema2001](#ref-BlanchardDykema2001)].

<a id="pm-h-10"></a>
#### The reduced adjunction

<a id="na:hnn-word"></a>
<a id="na:hnn-turns"></a>
Let $(A,\tau)$ be a unital $C^*$-algebra with faithful tracial state. Fix unital subalgebras $D_1,D_2\subset A$, trace-preserving conditional expectations $E_i:A\to D_i$, and a trace-preserving isomorphism $\theta:D_1\to D_2$. A word in $A$ and a unitary $v$ has the form $$\label{na:hnn-word}
 a_0v^{\epsilon_1}a_1\cdots v^{\epsilon_m}a_m,
 \qquad a_t\in A,\quad\epsilon_t\in\{+1,-1\}.$$ For $m\geq1$, call it *reduced* if every internal coefficient satisfies $$\label{na:hnn-turns}
 \begin{cases}
 E_1(a_t)=0,&(\epsilon_t,\epsilon_{t+1})=(+1,-1),\\
 E_2(a_t)=0,&(\epsilon_t,\epsilon_{t+1})=(-1,+1).
 \end{cases}$$ There is no condition at equal consecutive signs.

<a id="stmt-na-hnn"></a>
<div id="na:hnn" class="proposition">

**Proposition 12**. *Let $(A,\tau)$ be a unital $C^*$-algebra with faithful tracial state, let $D_1,D_2\subset A$ be unital subalgebras with trace-preserving conditional expectations $E_i:A\to D_i$, and let $\theta:D_1\to D_2$ be a trace-preserving isomorphism. There is a unital extension with a faithful tracial state extending $\tau$, $$H(A)=C^*(A,v),\qquad vdv^*=\theta(d)\quad(d\in D_1),$$ where $v$ is called the *stable unitary*, and a trace-preserving conditional expectation $E_A:H(A)\to A$ annihilating every nonempty reduced word <a href="#na:hnn-word" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="na:hnn-word">[na:hnn-word]</a>. Among faithfully tracial extensions generated by $A$ and such a unitary, the relation and vanishing of the trace on nonempty reduced words determine $H(A)$ uniquely, by an isomorphism fixing $A$ and $v$.*

</div>

We construct this adjunction as a matrix corner of an amalgamated free product. The proof below records the module and its two compression expectations; these will also be used in the pure-state bridge of Section <a href="#sec:bridge" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:bridge">4</a>.

<a id="pm-h-11"></a>
#### Construction from an amalgamated free product

An element is *centered* for a conditional expectation $E$ if it belongs to $\ker E$.

<a id="stmt-na-free-product"></a>
<div id="na:free-product" class="lemma">

<a id="na:free-core"></a>
**Lemma 13**. *Let $F$ and $G$ be unital $C^*$-algebras with faithful tracial states, containing a common unital subalgebra $D$ on which the traces agree. Suppose that $E_D^F:F\to D$ and $E_D^G:G\to D$ are trace-preserving conditional expectations. There is a unital $C^*$-algebra $Z$ generated by faithful copies of $F$ and $G$ agreeing on $D$, with a faithful tracial state extending both traces and faithful trace-preserving conditional expectations $$E_D:Z\longrightarrow D,
 \qquad E_F:Z\longrightarrow F,
 \qquad E_D=E_D^F\circ E_F.$$ The map $E_D$ vanishes on every nonempty alternating product of elements of $\ker E_D^F$ and $\ker E_D^G$. The map $E_F$ vanishes on every product $$\label{na:free-core}
 a_0 b_1 a_1\cdots b_m a_m,
 \qquad m\geq1,$$ where $a_0,a_m\in F$, $b_t\in\ker E_D^G$, and $a_t\in\ker E_D^F$ for $0<t<m$. The linear span of $F$ and these products is dense in $Z$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-na-free-product"><summary>Proof of stmt-na-free-product</summary>

<a id="na:free-module"></a>
*Proof.* For $i\in\{F,G\}$, complete $i$ as a right Hilbert $D$-module $L_i$ with inner product $$\langle x,y\rangle_D=E_D^i(x^*y).$$ Left multiplication by $a\in i$ is bounded, since $$E_D^i(x^*a^*ax)\leq\|a\|^2E_D^i(x^*x),$$ and its adjoint is left multiplication by $a^*$. The expectation gives an orthogonal decomposition $$L_i=D\oplus L_i^\circ,
 \qquad L_i^\circ=\overline{\ker E_D^i}.$$ Both summands are invariant under left and right multiplication by $D$. Form the Hilbert $D$-module $$\label{na:free-module}
 K=D\oplus
 \bigoplus_{\substack{m\geq1,\ i_t\in\{F,G\}\\i_t\ne i_{t+1}}}
 L_{i_1}^\circ\otimes_D\cdots\otimes_D L_{i_m}^\circ.$$ Here $\otimes_D$ is the interior tensor product of Hilbert modules; the left $D$-action on a word acts on its first factor. The first summand $D$ is called the vacuum summand, with distinguished vector $1$.

Let $K^{(i)}$ be the vacuum summand together with all words not starting with $i$. The decomposition of $L_i$ identifies $$K=L_i\otimes_D K^{(i)}.$$ Represent $i$ by left multiplication on the first tensor factor. The two representations agree on $D$. They are faithful: an element $a$ annihilating the vacuum satisfies $E_D^i(a^*a)=0$, so its faithful factor trace gives $a=0$. Let $Z$ be the $C^*$-algebra they generate in the adjointable operators on $K$.

A centered element of $i$ acting on $K^{(i)}$ adds its own tensor factor at the front. Thus a nonempty alternating centered product has zero vacuum compression. Centering arbitrary factor elements shows that $D$ and these products span a dense subspace of $Z$. Vacuum compression consequently takes values in the left copy of $D$ and defines the conditional expectation $E_D$.

Write $\tau_D$ for the common trace on $D$. We next prove that $\tau_Z=\tau_D\circ E_D$ is tracial and faithful. For traciality, compare $\tau_Z(aw)$ and $\tau_Z(wa)$ when $a$ belongs to one factor and $w$ is an alternating centered word. If $w$ has length at least two, both values vanish. Indeed, multiplication at a same-factor end, followed by centering that end product, leaves either an alternating centered word or a $D$-coefficient times a nonempty such word. At a different-factor end, first center $a$ and use $D$-bimodularity. For a one-letter word, use the factor trace when the factors agree; when they differ, both values are zero. For $w\in D$, use the factor trace. Density and repeated cyclic movement of a factor now prove traciality on all of $Z$.

Suppose $\tau_Z(z^*z)=0$. For every $t\in Z$, traciality gives $$0\leq\tau_Z(t^*z^*zt)
 =\tau_Z(ztt^*z^*)
 \leq\|t\|^2\tau_Z(zz^*)=0.$$ The trace on $D$ is faithful, so $E_D((zt)^*(zt))=0$. This is the $D$-valued inner product of $zt1$ with itself, and hence $zt1=0$. Products of centered letters create all elementary tensors in <a href="#na:free-module" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="na:free-module">[na:free-module]</a>; thus $Z1$ is dense in $K$. It follows that $z=0$, proving faithfulness of $\tau_Z$.

Finally, let $P_F$ be the orthogonal projection onto $D\oplus L_F^\circ=L_F$. This submodule reduces the $F$-action. A core $w=b_1a_1\cdots a_{m-1}b_m$ as in <a href="#na:free-core" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="na:free-core">[na:free-core]</a> sends $L_F$ into words starting with $G$, so $P_FwP_F=0$. Since $P_F$ commutes with $F$, arbitrary endpoint coefficients satisfy $$P_Fa_0wa_mP_F=a_0P_FwP_Fa_m=0.$$ Successive centering shows that $F$ and the products <a href="#na:free-core" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="na:free-core">[na:free-core]</a> span a dense subspace. Their compressions belong to the faithful left copy of $F$ on $L_F$, which is norm closed. Compression therefore defines a conditional expectation $E_F:Z\to F$. The vacuum lies in $L_F$, giving $E_D=E_D^F\circ E_F$ and trace preservation. Faithfulness follows from faithfulness of $\tau_Z$. ◻

</details>

<details id="proof-stmt-na-free-product-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-na-free-product</summary>

<a id="na:hnn-corner"></a>
*Proof of Proposition <a href="#na:hnn" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="na:hnn">12</a>.* Take $$F=M_2(A),\qquad G=M_2(D_1),\qquad D=D_1\oplus D_2.$$ Embed $D$ in $F$ by $(d_1,d_2)\mapsto\operatorname{diag}(d_1,d_2)$ and in $G$ by $(d_1,d_2)\mapsto\operatorname{diag}(d_1,\theta^{-1}(d_2))$. The normalized matrix traces agree on $D$, and the maps $$E_D^F(a)=(E_1(a_{11}),E_2(a_{22})),\qquad
 E_D^G(b)=(b_{11},\theta(b_{22}))$$ are trace-preserving conditional expectations.
